  # ASR-001 — Architecture Simplification Review (line 1)
    ## Scope (line 6)
    ## Executive Summary (line 10)
    ## 1. Are two capabilities solving the same problem? (line 21)
      ### Finding 1 — Investigation and deliberation overlap (line 23)
      ### Finding 2 — Judgement-understanding and deliberation overlap (line 41)
      ### Finding 3 — Reflection and memory overlap (line 56)
      ### Finding 4 — Compass, authority, and moral compass overlap at the governance layer (line 73)
    ## 2. Is any information stored twice? (line 90)
      ### Finding 5 — Reflection state is stored twice (line 92)
      ### Finding 6 — Deliberation state and judgement-understanding state partially duplicate the same recommendation (line 109)
      ### Finding 7 — Investigation results are re-expressed as deliberation findings and later as answer content (line 126)
      ### Finding 8 — Conversation state and memory both carry prior context (line 144)
    ## 3. Are there unnecessary processing stages? (line 158)
      ### Finding 9 — Investigation and deliberation could be treated as one reasoning stage (line 160)
      ### Finding 10 — Judgement-understanding is a presentation stage, not a separate cognition stage (line 170)
      ### Finding 11 — Reflection is not a second reasoning engine (line 180)
    ## 4. Are any theories implemented twice? (line 191)
      ### Finding 12 — The runtime implements theory-like structure in more than one place (line 193)
      ### Finding 13 — The runtime partly duplicates the role of the constitution in its own advisory flow (line 207)
    ## 5. Are names misleading? (line 217)
      ### Finding 14 — “Judgement understanding” is a misleading label for what the runtime is doing (line 219)
      ### Finding 15 — “Reflection” is broadly fine, but its relationship to memory needs clearer naming (line 233)
    ## 6. Is every runtime stage still justified? (line 239)
      ### Experience (line 241)
